# Feeds matrix Phase 2: evidence per firing cell

Date: 2026-09-23. Plan: `PLAN.md` sections 6 and 7. Matrix: `matrix_2026-09-23.csv`, `matrix_2026-09-23_sim.csv`.

## 1. Header

### Method

1. Thirteen read-only research classes (13 of 13 completed) compared each firing or formula-only cell with published figures.
2. The research fetched 122 unique published rows. Each finding then went to a verifier. The verifier fetched the URL again and quoted the row.
3. This reconciler merged the findings and the verifications into this file. No agent changed code or ran cargo.

### Counts

- Findings: 230.
- Confirmed by the verifier with a quote: 97.
- Not confirmed, with a URL: 72 (50 fetched, 22 not fetched).
- No URL (engine-internal rows, or rows where the search found no source): 61.
- Verdict `no_source_exists`: 42.
- All verdicts: `engine_low` 70, `internal_inconsistency` 49, `no_source_exists` 42, `agrees` 28, `not_comparable` 21, `engine_high` 10, `machine_fact` 10.

### Verification note (read before the tables)

The verifier's fetches were not stable. The verifiers report xref errors in the onsrud.com `images/*.pdf` files (pdfinfo title "PCT-19 Catalog.indd"), and different extractors returned different catalog pages. The parallel verifiers also used shared scratch file names (for example `out.pdf`, `onsrud.pdf`, `dl_1.pdf`), so one verifier can read another verifier's download. Several verifier downloads with different names (`dl_1.pdf` to `dl_4.pdf`, `onsrud.pdf`, `newfetch.pdf`) have the byte size of the Hard Wood file (638136). So one fetch of `Hard%20Wood.pdf` returned the Soft Wood sheet, and one fetch of the IDC short-url returned an Amana sheet.

The task rule is: put the verifier's figure in the table when its correction differs. The reconciler applied that rule only where the correction carries a real competing figure. The reconciler checked each disputed row against the Phase 2 local copies in `scratchpad/phase2/` with `pdftotext -layout`:

1. Fetch mismatch; the local copy shows the finding's figure. The finding's figure stays. The Verified column names the file and line. Rows (40): 2-3, 3.1-3, 3.1-4, 3.1-6, 3.1-7, 3.1-15, 3.2-9, 3.2-14, 3.2-15, 3.2-16, 3.2-17, 3.3-1, 3.3-3, 3.4-1, 3.4-2, 3.4-3, 3.4-4, 3.4-5, 3.4-8, 3.4-9, 3.4-12, 3.4-13, 3.5-3, 3.5-4, 4.1-7, 4.1-18, 4.1-19, 4.2-12, 4.3-6, 4.3-9, 4.3-11, 4.3-12, 4.3-13, 5.1-4, 5.1-11, 5.2-12, 5.2-18, 5.2-19, 6-3, 6-9.
2. Real competing figure. The verifier's figure goes in the table; the Notes column keeps "finding said ...". Rows (3): 3.4-10, 4.3-7, 6-11.
3. The verifier compared a derived ramp-down value with the printed full-feed chip load. Both figures are in the table. Rows (4): 3.1-10, 3.1-12, 3.1-13, 3.1-14.
4. A condition caveat with no figure change. Rows (4): 3.1-5, 3.5-9, 3.5-15, 6-10.
5. No local copy to arbitrate. The verifier's result stands as "not confirmed". Rows (2): 3.4-17, 4.1-24.

The local copies are in `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-ricky-personal-repos-rs-cam/0ce61648-f7af-45d1-ba8a-1d09d9291459/scratchpad/phase2/`. That directory belongs to one session and is temporary. The overrides above depend on these files: `Hard%20Wood.pdf`, `MDF.pdf`, `Hard%20Plywood.pdf`, `o_Soft%20Wood.pdf`, `onsrud_drill.pdf`, `idc.pdf` (`idc.txt`), `amana_v24.pdf`, `amana_zrn3d.pdf`, `amana_zrn3d_v8.pdf`, `freud.pdf`, `lmt_guide.pdf`, `shapeoko_chart.pdf`, `sienci_imp.pdf`.

Consequence for the programme: a URL is not a stable source for these vendors. The manifest needs a stored copy (or a hash) of each PDF, not only the URL.

### Corrections to PLAN.md (from the evidence map)

1. PLAN section 3 counts 256 LUT rows (exact 228, flat_end x pocket 65). Those counts include `data/vendor_lut/industrial_only/` (4 rows). `VendorLut::embedded()` does not load that folder. Loaded: 252 rows (exact 224, derived 15, fallback 13), 58 cells, flat_end x pocket 61. Non-wood rows are 67, not 62.
2. PLAN section 6a names "Onsrud 77-100" as a finishing row. The LUT has no Onsrud 77-100 row. The Onsrud sheets do print 77-100 in Hard Wood, Soft Wood, MDF and Hard Plywood (not Soft Plywood). `source_manifest.json` says the opposite (see row 3.5-1 and the TaperedBallNose gaps).
3. Two rigidity default sets exist. `RigidityProfile::default()` (machine/mod.rs:146) is 0.25 / 0.10 / 2.0. `generic_wood_router()` (:271) is 0.20 / 0.08 / 1.5, and `MachineProfile::default()` returns it. The matrix used the second set.
4. The INTEGRATION.md family table is stale. It says Waterline = Parallel/Finish and SteepShallow = Parallel. The registry says Waterline = Contour/SemiFinish and SteepShallow = Contour/Finish (catalog/registry.rs:1004).

### How to read the tables

Each row is one finding. The bold label (for example **3.1-4**) is the row reference that section 8 uses. Engine value carries the engine file:line in parentheses. Ratio is engine / published unless the cell says otherwise. Finding text is transcribed as the research agents wrote it.

The principle (one chip load per diameter for square and corner-radius noses) has vendor support in metal only. No wood vendor sanctions flat-end data for a corner-radius tool. Amana sells a corner-radius wood bit (46460 / 46460-K, 1/16 R x 1/4 D) but the v27 plunge chart omits it and the product pages return HTTP 403. Whiteside publishes no chip-load card (sources.toml:276-290). |

I searched each PDF for 'bull', 'corner', 'radius' and the row numbers; nothing matched. The rows sit in an Amana file but cite Onsrud. For comparison, Onsrud 52-200 1/4 in hard wood is .005-.007 in (0.127-0.178 mm), 2.1-4x the bull hardwood row. The d6.0 hardwood Adaptive cell uses this row and still clamps to the 0.025 floor. |

The effective diameter does not change any CSV feed: the chip-thinning factor is reported only, and the power check (the other reader) does not fire. Kennametal lists an interactive 'Ball Nose Surface Finish' calculator (sources.toml kennametal_metals) but no bull arm was read. |

## 4. Firing ids (four classes)

### 4.1 Firing id: feeds.chipload_clamped_to_floor

Rows: 26. Verdicts: `engine_low` 14, `no_source_exists` 5, `not_comparable` 3, `internal_inconsistency` 3, `agrees` 1.

| Cells | Material | Engine value (file:line) | Published figure (source, URL, row, condition) | Ratio | Verdict | Verified | Notes |

The chart gives chipload bands and a depth rule only. Its smallest wood figure is .001 in/tooth = 0.0254 mm (series 64-000/65-000, 1/16 in, low edge of a recommended band). Source: LMT Onsrud, Hard Wood cutting data chart (same layout in Soft Wood, MDF, Hard/Soft Plywood). URL: https://www.onsrud.com/images/Hard%20Wood.pdf. Row: "DEPTH OF CUT: 1 x D Use recommended chip load / 2 x D Reduce chip load by 25% / 3 x D Reduce chip load by 50%"; "FORMULAS: Chip Load = Feed Rate / (RPM x # of cutting edges)"; 64-000/65-000 1/16: .001-.003. Condition: Onsrud wood charts, per series and cutting diameter, 1xD depth | n/a | no_source_exists | not verified: the verifier did not fetch this URL | The code cites an "Onsrud min-chip-thickness rule". The Onsrud chart has no such rule. A grep for minimum/small/heat/dull in the PDF text finds nothing. The cutting-data index page (https://onsrud.com/Forms/Cutting-Data-Recommendations.asp) holds no text on the subject. The 0.0254 mm band edge at 1/16 in is equal to the constant. Verify whether this band edge is the origin. It is a recommended band minimum for one small series, not a floor for all diameters. |

Source: Amana Tool, 2 Flute Solid Carbide V-Groove Engraving 15deg, 60deg & 90deg Router Bits speed chart. The comparison uses the Amana 60deg 2-flute wood chart. The chart is internally inconsistent: its printed chipload column equals the 1-flute arithmetic (50/18000 = 0.0028 in, 125/18000 = 0.0069 in), which is 2x its own 2-flute formula. The engine target agrees with the IPM-derived band and sits 3 % under the printed band. Amana prints the same figure for soft and hard wood; the engine's 1.56x soft/hard split comes from its own hardness exponent q=1.26, not from this source. A second 60deg 2F Amana chart (AMS-159, carbide-tipped 45707): 90 IPM -> 0.0635 mm derived (0.0762 printed); engine/derived = 1.17. |

## 5. Post-simulation and worked examples

### 5.1 Post-simulation, worked example (a): the depth gate on finishes

Rows: 15. Verdicts: `internal_inconsistency` 5, `no_source_exists` 4, `not_comparable` 3, `engine_low` 2, `agrees` 1.

| Cells | Material | Engine value (file:line) | Published figure (source, URL, row, condition) | Ratio | Verdict | Verified | Notes |

| **5.1-1** EndMill/Waterline d6.0 (sim, dome fixture); also BallNose/Scallop, TaperedBallNose/Scallop, EndMill/BallNose/TaperedBallNose DropCutter d6.0 (sim) | softwood, hardwood (sim subset) | Waterline peak 4.7786 mm, Scallop ball 7.1960 mm, Scallop taper 7.0154 mm, DropCutter end 2.1423 / ball 1.4472 / taper 1.4060 mm; peaks identical in softwood and hardwood (tool_load/depth.rs:176-198 (peak = max axial_engagement_mm over steady cutting samples); dexel_stock/simulation.rs:1081-1100 (axial_engagement_mm = measured_axial_mm); dexel_stock/stamping.rs:1783,1801,1826-1828 and dexel_stock/swept.rs:711,728 (max_penetration = max per-cell pre_len - post_len of one stamp); tests/feeds_matrix_instrument_fm1.rs:647-690 (single finishing op on fresh 18 mm stock, no rough)) | none for this quantity; a published finishing ap applies to a pass over pre-roughed stock with a finish allowance. Source: n/a (instrument reading). Condition: 6 mm tools, 2 flutes, dome 46 mm on base z-8, stock top 0 / bottom -18, sim resolution 1.0, no roughing op before the finish | n/a | not_comparable | not verified: no URL (engine-internal or searched-and-absent row) | Answer to task (i). peak_mm is the tallest material column one stamp removes in one dexel cell. It is not the wall contact height and not the z_step. The peaks do not change with material, so they are geometry, not load. The fixture runs each finishing recipe on unroughed stock, so the column is stock-top-to-surface material that no rough removed. On this fixture every 3D depth verdict measures roughing removal with a finish recipe; the ratios 4.78/0.48 = 9.96 and 7.20/0.48 = 15.0 are ratios of the wrong quantity. On a roughed vertical wall each waterline level removes a column of about z_step (1.0 mm) plus the leave, so the gate would still read about z_step (see the next finding). |

| **5.1-6** EndMill,BullNose/Profile d6.0 (sim), 8 of 8 cells | all | depth peak printed 1.2000 mm, cap 1.2000 mm, verdict Exceeds (tool_load/depth.rs:201 (exceeds_high(peak, cap, 0.0)); tool_load/boundary.rs:96 (BOUNDARY_EPSILON_REL = 8 x f64::EPSILON) and :105-108 (inclusive, observed > bound + slack); stock/dexel.rs:17,19 (DexelSegment enter/exit f32), :197 (ray_material_length -> f32); dexel_stock/stamping.rs:1783,1801,1826 (f32 lengths cast to f64 and differenced); feeds/suggest/invariants.rs:379 (dpp = cap exactly)) | n/a (engine versus engine). The compare is inclusive, so exact equality reads Within (depth.rs:380-387 test). The observation is an f32 dexel difference. An f32 reproduction of 18-mm ray lengths cut in 1.2 mm levels gives removed columns of 1.2000007629 mm on levels 1, 3, 8, 13 and 15; that is 7.6e-7 mm above the cap and prints as 1.2000. The 8-ulp f64 slack (about 2.1e-15 mm here) was sized against a 1-ulp f64 multiply/divide census (boundary.rs:67-95), not against f32 storage, whose ulp near 18 mm is about 1.9e-6 mm. Probable cause, consistent with the printed values; not verified by a rerun (forbidden). The engine's own clamped recipe (dpp = cap) trips its own gate by rounding. |

The verifier result supports the finding's point: the LUT row has no printed source. The CSV confirms the chain mechanics: bounds scale on diameter only, and there is no DOC derate at this cell. But the band's lookup diameter comes from DOC = D = 3.175 mm (the unwrap_or default), while the same result reports axial depth 0.3175 mm. At 0.3175 mm the engaged diameter is 1.905 mm (ball region), which gives scale 0.732 and a band of 0.0088-0.0176. Suggest's band and the gate's band (which uses the peak axial DOC, tool_load/chipload.rs:535-551) therefore scale one row from two different diameters for one cut. The gate verdict on the simulated d6.0 dome cell was Within (matrix_2026-09-23_sim.csv), so no trip is shown. This finding comes from reading the code and matching the CSV numbers to four digits. No test ran it. |

## 7. Gaps

Each research class wrote its own gaps text. The text is transcribed without change.

### 2 Refused cells (scallop family) (`refused-scallop-family`)

No tooling vendor (Amana, Onsrud, Whiteside, Freud, Techno, PreciseBits) publishes a statement that a scallop or 3D finishing pass REQUIRES a ball or tapered ball. The published statements are CAM-vendor recommendations (Autodesk Fusion, Vectric), not prohibitions. The Onsrud cutting-data PDFs hold no ball or tapered-ball rows at all (source_manifest.json:49), so Onsrud gives nothing on this question. IDC Woodcraft finishing guidance (MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md:296) names ball and taper-ball stepovers only and says nothing about flat or V tools on a finish. No source names a spiral finish specifically. I did not re-fetch the Amana Spektra v6 or ZrN v8 PDFs; the flat-bottom row figures come from the in-tree transcription and the LUT observations. Side observation outside this class: EndMill DropCutter cells report support_arm VendorBacked although the LUT has no flat_end x parallel row; the orchestrator should check which row backs them. URLs used, accessed 2026-09-23: https://docs.vectric.com/docs/V12.0/VCarvePro/ENU/Help/form/Finish%20Machining%20Toolpath/index.html ; https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/ENU/Fusion-CAM/files/3D-SCALLOP-STEPS.htm ; web search for Amana 3D carving ball nose vs flat end mill (product pages only, no statement found; for example https://www.toolstoday.com/v-12280-ams-143.html sells a '2D and 3D Carving Ball Nose & Flat Bottom' set, which implies flat-bottom tools are used in 3D carving).

### 3.1 Formula-only: EndMill (`formula-only-EndMill`)

1) No published chip load exists for a flat end mill on a shallow finishing, trace, chamfer, pencil or V-carve pass in wood. Every comparison above uses a 1xD chart (Amana Spektra spiral plunge v24, Onsrud 52-200/57-200). A shallower pass has no published increase or decrease. 2) No published stepover or finishing depth exists for a flat end mill in wood. The in-tree and web searches found only community forum figures. 3) The drill comparator is the Amana ramp-down rule (Feed IPM / flutes). No straight-plunge figure for a spiral end mill in wood was found. Onsrud 72-000 is a boring drill and is not comparable. FPL-GTR-282 has no drilling content. 4) The provenance of the in-tree LUT rows attributed to amana_spektra_spiral_plunge_v24 is doubtful. I fetched the chart on 2026-09-23 from https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/Solid-Carbide-Spektra-Spiral-Plunge-2-3-Flute-v24.pdf. It prints Wood/Plywood 1/8" .0040 in (0.1016 mm) and 6 mm .0050 in (0.127 mm), with no hardwood/softwood split and no finish rows. Yet amana_flat_end.json carries amana-flat-hardwood-contour-3175-2f (0.018-0.030 mm, finish, evidence_grade a, row_kind exact) and adaptive/pocket rows (for example softwood 3.175 0.032-0.048 mm) that cite this chart but do not appear on it. The amana_long_tail spektra rows (3 mm softwood 0.1016) do match it. This is outside this class; it needs its own row. 5) The CSV has no peck-depth column, so the drill peck (repo-authored bands) was not compared. 6) No cell of this class is in matrix_2026-09-23_sim.csv, so the depth-cap inconsistency is a pre-simulation finding only. 7) Onsrud publishes 1/4 in, not 6 mm. The 6 mm cells use the 1/4 in rows, a 5.8 % diameter difference. The Amana chart has an exact 6 mm row. Scratch PDFs are in /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ricky-personal-repos-rs-cam/0ce61648-f7af-45d1-ba8a-1d09d9291459/scratchpad/phase2/ (spektra_plunge_v24.pdf, onsrud_drill.pdf).

### 3.2 Formula-only: BallNose (`formula-only-BallNose`)

1) No vendor labels a ball-nose ROUGHING, adaptive or pocketing row. The Amana ball chart v7 and ZrN v8 are 1xD charts. IDC lists the ball nose in its ENDMILL table (40% stepover imperial, 20% metric; the source disagrees with itself). 2) No vendor publishes a ball-nose plywood row, a waterline or contour-finish ae/ap for a ball in wood, a light-finish chipload for wood, or a ball-nose drilling row. Onsrud publishes no ball rows at any diameter. 3) LUT metadata, outside this class: source_manifest.json calls the amana_ball_nose_v7 3.175/6.0 rows 'derived (reduced) for 3D-finish use', but the observations mark them row_kind exact, grade a. They sit about 6x below the chart fetched 2026-09-23 (for example softwood 3.175: LUT 0.018-0.030 mm vs chart 0.127-0.178 mm). 4) The post-sim subset has no ball Waterline, Pocket or Adaptive cell, so the depth and chipload verdicts for this class are pre-simulation. 5) Spektra 3D v6 was fetched but not used as a comparator: it has no 2F 1/8 in ball row, and its 2F 1/4 in row repeats ZrN v8 (0.007-0.009 in). 6) Every feed ratio carries the instrument's LD derate (0.75 at Ø3.175, 0.88 at Ø6) and the 0.75 safety factor. The full JSON is at /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ricky-personal-repos-rs-cam/0ce61648-f7af-45d1-ba8a-1d09d9291459/scratchpad/phase2/formula_only_ballnose.json. URLs used (accessed 2026-09-23): https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/Spiral-Ball-Nose-Speed-Chart-v7.pdf ; https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/ZrN-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart-v8.pdf ; https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/Spektra-Coated-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart-v6.pdf ; https://community.carbide3d.com/uploads/short-url/fwPIYiWQNjUx8eEwsA7qmYiLMxv.pdf ; https://toolstoday.com/learn/3d-carving-ball-nose-router-bits (no numeric figure). The Vectric, PreciseBits and DAPRA figures come from MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md. This session did not fetch them again.

### 3.3 Formula-only: BullNose (`formula-only-BullNose`)

1. No vendor (Amana, Onsrud, Whiteside, Freud) publishes a wood chip load, stepover or depth for a bull-nose or corner-radius tool. Amana sells one (46460 / 46460-K). Its product pages return HTTP 403, and the v27 spiral-plunge chart does not list it. 2. Every published comparison band is a 1xD square-end chart. The engine cells are shallow finish passes (ap/D 0.06-0.10). The per-tooth ratios are valid, but the depth condition is different. 3. The simulation CSV has no bull finish rows (2D square fixture only). The depth internal_inconsistency (finish ap above the 0.08 D cap) is therefore a prediction from the shared cap producer, not a measured Exceeds. 4. I did not download the Onsrud Soft Plywood PDF. 5. The Onsrud 60-200 'Finishing' series prints no 1/8 in value, so d3.175 is compared against 52-200. 6. The Kennametal ball-nose finish calculator is interactive only; I read no bull-nose cusp figure. Sources I read (access 2026-09-23): onsrud.com/images/{Hard%20Wood,Soft%20Wood,MDF,Hard%20Plywood}.pdf; onsrud.com/Forms/Cutting-Data-Recommendations.asp; amanatool.com multi-helix-feed-speed-chart-v3.pdf, Spektra-Coated-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart-v6.pdf, Solid-Carbide-Spektra-Spiral-Plunge-2-3-Flute-v27.pdf. Scratch copies are in /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ricky-personal-repos-rs-cam/0ce61648-f7af-45d1-ba8a-1d09d9291459/scratchpad/phase2/.

### 3.4 Formula-only: VBit (`formula-only-VBit`)

> Correction to `MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md` section 5.4 is in this text: the IDC columns are 'Side Angle, Feed, Plunge', and the check read the side angle as the feed.

No manufacturer (Amana, Whiteside, Onsrud, Freud) publishes a V-bit figure for clearing, adaptive, or 3D finishing. The Amana V-groove and engraving charts are trace/engraving rows. The research found no Amana flat-bottom V clearing chart. Vectric documents the V-bit 'Clearance Pass Stepover' and 'Final Pass Stepover' fields but publishes no values (https://docs.vectric.com/docs/V9.5/VCarvePro/ENU/Help/Reference%20Manual/Toolpaths/Tool%20Database/Tool%20Database.html). The only numeric V-bit clearing and final-pass figures are retailer and hobby sources: IDC Woodcraft (1/4in 60 deg only) and Sienci (60/90 deg V-carving, no diameter or flute count). The Sienci V-bit stepdown columns (0.42/0.92 in softwood) exceed the cone height of any 60 deg V-bit, so the depth comparisons do not use them. The research found no 1/2in 60 deg clearing figure, no V-bit drilling figure, and no per-material split (IDC gives one row for all woods). The simulation table has no VBit cells, so no post-simulation load or depth verdict exists for this class. CORRECTION to MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md §5.4: the IDC PDF columns are 'Side Angle, Feed, Plunge'. The check read the side angle (30 for the 60 deg bit, 15 for 30 deg, 45 for 90 deg) as the feed. The real 60 deg feed is 60 in/min = 1524 mm/min (p.17, p.22, p.27; p.11 says 40 in/min), plunge 20 in/min, so the chip is 0.0346 mm/t (0.00136 in), not 0.00068 in. The IDC-to-Amana gap is therefore about 2.2-5x, not 4-10x. That section needs a correction row. Sources accessed 2026-09-23: https://community.carbide3d.com/uploads/short-url/fwPIYiWQNjUx8eEwsA7qmYiLMxv.pdf ; https://resources.sienci.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Feeds-and-Speeds-Imperial.pdf ; the Vectric doc URL above ; Amana chart URLs as cited in MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md. The scratch PDFs are in /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ricky-personal-repos-rs-cam/0ce61648-f7af-45d1-ba8a-1d09d9291459/scratchpad/phase2/.

### 3.5 Formula-only: TaperedBallNose (`formula-only-TaperedBallNose`)

1. Onsrud 77-100 (tapered ball) is not in the LUT. The cause is on record: source_manifest.json entry onsrud_cutting_data_recommendations says the Onsrud wood PDFs 'do NOT include any ball nose or tapered ball nose entries at any diameter'. That is false. pdftotext of Hard Wood, Soft Wood, MDF and Hard Plywood (fetched 2026-09-23) shows the row '77-100 1xD .003-.005 .005-.007' in each; Soft Plywood has none. 77-102 = 1/8 in CD, 1/16 in radius, 1 deg per side, 3 flutes (https://toolsxp.com/products/onsrud-77-102), so the column diameter is the tip.
2. The 10 amana-tapered-* LUT rows (observations/amana_3d_profiling.json, grade a, row_kind exact, cited to ZrN-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart.pdf) cannot be traced to that PDF. Neither the v7 PDF nor v8 (-v8.pdf) contains 'taper', 'hardwood', 'softwood', ap or ae. Both print one 'Wood, MDF, Sign-Foam' row per tool. The manifest's own note says the chart does not split softwood from hardwood. The chart's 2-flute ball columns are 1 mm, 1/16 in and 6 mm-1/4 in (0.007-0.009 in = 0.178-0.229 mm at 6 mm-1/4 in); its tool-reference list includes tapered carving bits (46256, 46470, 46471). These rows belong to the vendor-backed class; they are recorded here because they were the in-tree comparison. The PLAN 3.1 figure 'tapered_ball_nose parallel 8, scallop 2' rests on them.
3. No FormulaOnly tapered cell is in matrix_2026-09-23_sim.csv. The depth verdicts use the CSV cap columns and the two vendor-backed tapered sim rows (Scallop, DropCutter d6.0, depth Exceeds 7.02 / 1.41 vs cap 0.72).
4. PreciseBits publishes no feed rate (it sends the user to its Sweetspot Test). Its 3 % chip rule is a calibration start point, not a tapered-tool figure.
5. Amana product pages (e.g. 46256) returned HTTP 403; Onsrud series page gave no table. IDC and ShopBot figures come from MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md with their URLs; I did not re-fetch them.
6. No published figure exists for a 7 deg tapered ball at a 6 mm tip; the Onsrud 1/4 in column is 6.35 mm and 1 deg per side.
7. The Adaptive d6.0 chip carries a reduction beyond safety x L/D; the CSV does not name it and I did not trace it.
URLs used (accessed 2026-09-23): https://onsrud.com/images/Hard%20Wood.pdf ; https://onsrud.com/images/Soft%20Wood.pdf ; https://onsrud.com/images/MDF.pdf ; https://onsrud.com/images/Hard%20Plywood.pdf ; https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/ZrN-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart.pdf ; https://www.amanatool.com/pub/media/productattachments/ZrN-3D-Profiling-Feed-Chip-Load-Chart-v8.pdf ; https://www.precisebits.com/products/carbidebits/taperedcarve250b2f.asp ; https://www.precisebits.com/tutorials/calibrating_feeds_n_speeds.htm ; https://toolsxp.com/products/onsrud-77-102 ; https://onsrud.com/Series/SolidCarbideTaperedBallNose.asp (no table). ### 4.2 Firing id: efficiency.very_fine_stepover and the finish stepover (`id-very_fine_stepover-and-finish-stepover`)

Material: the stepover in this class has no material term. operation_default_profile (feeds/mod.rs:2570-2660) and the finish overrides (:2756-2790) take no material input outside the Adaptive branch. The four materials give identical stepovers, so each row uses material "all". No source splits finishing stepover by wood species either. Load and depth: this class has no load or depth verdict, so the sim CSV was not used. Instrument conditions: SuggestContext::default() has no model_bbox, so backoff_stepover_for_runtime does not act. A real project can raise these stepovers before the check runs. DropCutter with scallop_height set takes the stepover from geometry::scallop_stepover (Step 3b) and not from the profile table; the instrument did not walk that path. Not found: no wood vendor (Amana, Onsrud, Whiteside, Freud) publishes a finishing stepover for any tool family. The three Amana PDFs were grepped and hold only the 1/2/3 x D depth rule. No published flat, bull nose or V-bit raster finishing stepover exists. No published scallop-height target exists. The IDC taper 5-8 % basis (cut D or tip D) is not stated. The CNCCookbook "1/8" figure came from a WebFetch summary and was not checked word for word. DAPRA was not re-fetched; the text is from MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md. The ToolsToday and CNCCookbook pages are general guidance, not wood-vendor charts. URLs accessed 2026-09-23: Vectric V12 finish page, PreciseBits taperedcarve250b2f, IDC PDF (carbide3d short-url), cnccookbook.com/cnc-stepover, toolstoday.com 3D carving article, Amana Spektra v24, Spiral Ball Nose v7 and ZrN 3D profiling PDFs. ### 4.3 Firing ids: machine limits (shank, feed ceiling, drill envelope) (`id-machine-limits-shank-feed-drill`)

1. The CSV does not carry the FeedRateClamped.requested or DrillFeedClampedToEnvelope.requested values. The unclamped feeds above are derived as rpm x r_chip_load_mm x flutes (raw, Step 5), then x0.75 (Step 9). This works because overhang is None and workholding is Medium, so the Step-5b factors are 1.0 (feeds/mod.rs:1859-1886), and depth_tier is 1 at these depths. A power-ladder change was not excluded; one run with the warning values printed confirms the numbers.
2. Open drill RPM question, possibly an internal inconsistency; not given a verdict. The Step 9c follow-down (feeds/mod.rs:2466-2486) sets rpm = max(rpm x clamped/requested, band floor 8000) when the ceiling binds. By that arithmetic, d3.175 softwood should ship at 8000 rpm, not the CSV's 9299, and d6 softwood at about 8940, not 10158. At the CSV rpm the commanded chipload is 0.068 mm/tooth (d3.175) against the 0.091 target. The instrument reads the operation's spindle_rpm (tests/feeds_matrix_instrument_fm1.rs:391/697, written by feeds/suggest/apply.rs:147). This agent could not tell without running cargo whether Suggest overwrites the calculator rpm, or whether the follow-down does not run. Next step: print FeedsResult.rpm for one d3.175 softwood Drill cell.
3. The milling Step 7 clamp keeps the RPM. The commanded chipload on the 30 feed_clamped cells therefore drops to 0.27-0.50 x the Onsrud minimum. The sim CSV still reports load.chipload.within for the d6 hardwood/mdf Profile cells. This agent did not trace which band the post-sim chipload gate uses, because that is outside this class.
4. Onsrud wood router charts print no RPM. The 18000 rpm used for 'implied feed' is ShopBot's convention for Onsrud data (MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md:121); the rows also give the implied feed at the engine's own rpm.
5. No published drill row exists for BallNose, BullNose, TaperedBallNose or VBit used as drills. The Onsrud 72-000 comparison applies strictly only to a boring drill. The rows use it as an order-of-magnitude figure for EndMill and flag the others.
6. Amana 46xxx: this agent confirmed 46282, 46287, 46294 and 46490 (all 1/4 in shank) through search results. It did not read a complete Amana tapered-ball catalogue PDF. It found no 1/8 in shank PreciseBits tapered-ball specification page, only a mention.

### 4.4 Firing ids: V-bit rows, ProjectCurve routing, compatibility advisories (`id-vbit-rows-projectcurve-compat`)

1. The derivation of k0/p/q (reference/shapeoko_feeds_and_speeds/PARAMETER_DERIVATION.md) exists only in a gitignored directory (.gitignore:24). Onsrud MDF/Plywood/Hard Wood/Soft Wood figures come from the in-tree transcription (MACHINIST_REFERENCE_CHECK.md, no access date). I did not re-fetch those PDFs. For the matrix TaperedBallNose cell, I inferred from the exact numeric match that Scallop's operation_feeds_hints gives no axial hint. I did not read operation_feeds_hints line by line. 7. Ball, Bull, VBit and TaperedBall FormulaOnly cells use the same formula at the engaged diameter. No publisher gives a chip load for those families that ties to this formula, so I compared only the end-mill values. ## 8. Bearing on the rulings

This section lists the rows that bear on each ruling in PLAN section 7. It gives no recommendation.

### R1. Does a formula-only recommendation ship?

- The formula's origin: k0 and p fit the community Shapeoko A-to-Z soft-wood table; the fit record is in a gitignored file (6-1, 6-2, 3.1-24). q does not reproduce that table's hard-wood column (6-4).
- The formula against vendor charts: below Onsrud and Amana on most cells (3.1-1, 3.1-2, 3.1-5, 3.1-6, 3.2-1, 3.2-2, 3.3-1, 3.3-5, 3.5-1, 3.5-2, 6-6, 6-7); above the Carbide 3D measured chart (6-3, 6-5).
- The formula with the engine's own derates falls below the engine's own floor by construction (3.1-17, 3.3-9, 4.1-5, 4.1-17).
- Vendor-backed rows are not always better sourced: rows not on the cited chart (4.1-12, 4.1-15, 5.1-10, 5.2-19, 6-11); bull rows that cite a page with no bull row (3.3-16, 3.3-17).
- Recipes that ship next to the engine's own "wrong tool" flag (3.1-22, 3.5-19, 4.4-15, 3.5-18, 4.2-13); refusal routing and wording (2-1, 2-6, 2-7).

### R2. The depth criterion on radial-load finishes (PLAN 6a)

- What the gate measures: the removed column on unroughed stock, not the z-step (5.1-1).
- Suggest ships finish depths above its own finish cap, because the clamp acts on roughing only (5.1-2, 3.1-19, 3.2-10, 3.3-13, 3.5-15).
- Cap arithmetic: tip versus shaft diameter on a tapered tool (5.1-7, 3.5-15); peak = cap reads Exceeds through f32 storage (5.1-6, 5.2-16); depth Exceeds is absent from the diagnostics list (5.1-15); Unmodeled gates reported as `*.within` (5.1-14).
- Published figures: no vendor states a waterline or finish axial cap for wood (5.1-3, 5.1-12, 5.1-13); peripheral depth is a multiple of D (5.1-4); the one radial finish rule is 20 % of the roughing tool diameter (5.1-5); PreciseBits 1x / 2x tip (5.1-8, 3.5-8); DAPRA is metal (5.1-9, 4.2-15).

### R3. The 1.5 x D static hint versus the Onsrud depth rule (PLAN 6b)

- The hint has no source and fired on 0 of 960 cells (5.2-1); its relation to the published rule (5.2-2).
- On the two Shapeoko presets the hint fires after the engine applies the correct 0.75 derate (code reading, not measured) (5.2-3).
- The feed derate agrees with Onsrud, Freud and Techno at the printed points (5.2-4, 5.2-8, 5.2-9, 5.2-10, 5.2-11, 5.2-12, 5.2-13, 5.2-15, 5.1-11, 3.2-8).
- Two derate functions disagree between the printed points; the chipload_bounds column never carries the depth rule (5.2-6, 5.2-7).

### R4. De-rate vocabulary: which published rules may scale a figure

- "Reduce chip load" and "reduce feed rate" are one rule at fixed RPM; Amana changed the word between editions (5.2-17). Freud says "at least" (5.2-18).
- Scales with no source: 0.45 above 3 x D (5.2-5); the L/D factors and the 0.75 safety factor (5.2-14); the rigidity cap factors (5.1-12, 3.3-14); the drill multiplier 2.5 and the envelope (6-8, 3.1-16, 4.3-14).
- The DOC derate applied to a tapered tool at its engaged cone diameter explains the x0.704 of H1 (6-10); Suggest and the gate scale one row from two diameters (6-11, 3.5-7).
- The floor moves a mid-band recipe to the band maximum (4.1-6); the floor's three citations do not state 0.025 mm (4.1-1, 4.1-2, 4.1-3, 4.1-4, 4.1-26).

### R5. Coverage gaps to fill from the reference check

- Onsrud 77-100 (tapered ball) is in four wood sheets and not in the LUT (3.5-1, 3.5-2, 3.5-3, 3.5-4, 4.1-14, 4.1-16); no Onsrud plywood PDF is in the manifest (5.2-11, 3.3-4).
- Every chart puts MDF above wood; the engine puts it below (3.1-3, 5.2-10).
- Ball rows reduced about 6x with no named source (4.1-12, 4.1-8); flat_end Spektra rows not on the chart (5.2-19).
- V-bit: RPM-only Whiteside anchor (4.4-1, 4.4-5); no MDF or plywood V-bit row (4.4-3, 4.4-4, 4.1-25); nominal-D pricing and a 2.2x RPM split on one tool (3.4-6, 3.4-13, 4.4-7); Adaptive depth past the 60° cone (3.4-8).
- Families with no published row at all: bull finishing and ProjectCurve (3.3-16, 4.4-8, 4.4-6); tapered ball on trace, chamfer, drill (3.5-14, 3.5-17, 3.5-18); ball drilling (3.2-18).

No number in the engine was changed by this phase.
